 Mama Sol Mama Africa (dub) (6:00)
 Review: Although best-known these days for serving up blissful Balearic beats, Chris Coco's Coco, Steel & Lovebomb project started life as a platform for his early 90s house productions. Pleasingly, the veteran British producer is now making house again, in this instance in cahoots with long-serving Italian artist DJ Rocca. On side A, they both deliver their own pass on 'Echoes In My Mind', with Rocca's fluid, warm and organic Italian dream house-meets-Balearic house pass being followed by Coco's hybrid deep house/nu-disco take. They jointly explore their love of African musical culture on languid, hazy and mid-tempo B-side 'Mama Sol Mama Africa', where fluttering flutes, warm Rhodes keys and layered percussion catch the ear. That comes backed with a lovely alternative 'dub' full of effects-laden musical details and ear-catching percussion hits. … Read more
 Played by: [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/chart/34552042] Chris Coco , [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/chart/33795904] Juno Recommends Deep House , [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/chart/35492308] DJ ROCCA

 Warp (3:22)
 Review: Noted synthesiser fetishist and boogie revivalist Brian Ellis excels at delivering unlikely and often radically different cover versions, as his 2015 synth-funk flip of Steely Dan's 'Peg' proves. Here the California-based multi-instrumentalist is back on Tugboat Editions with another pair of inspired covers. He first re-imagines Gary Numan classic 'Metal' as a moody chunk of sci-fi electro, before having his wicked way with British new wave synth-pop act New Musik's 1982 jam 'Warp'. Ellis's version is even more futurist and intergalactic than the band's original version, with vocoder vocals riding a squelchy, metronomic bassline, deep cosmos chords and sharp synth melodies. … Read more

 Groovy Feeling (FAFF remix) (5:48)
 Review: Acid electro, big beat, chillout and everything in between: UK 90s dance hydrants Fluke present the remix companion to The Second Bite, the June 2026 slap-up reworking and finishing off a formerly unorganised library of material - "a testament to never selling a sampler or synthesiser". Fluke are known for their substantial contribution to the rave and chillout soundsystems of the 90s and 00s, predominantly as remixers of post-punk holdovers such as Talk Talk and New Order. Now the good karma revolves, their own originals like 'Electric Guitar' and 'Atom Bomb' remixed by warehouse-filling favourites and new faces alike, from Daniel Avery to Steve Cobby to Justin Robertson. … Read more

 Dr Doctor (instrumental mix) (3:26)
 Review: Edinburgh label Hobbes Music's recently launched Noetic Rhythm appears to have been established as an outlet for more out-there, experimental and pleasingly unusual fare. That's certainly a fitting description for Scottish artist George Demure aka George T's The Melodica Sessions, a fusion of digi-dub, Weatherall-esque post-punk fuzziness, and the artist's own poetic spoken word vocals. It's not brand-new - many of the tracks first surfaced digitally years ago - but is well worth this expanded vinyl edition. There's plenty to savour, from the Melodica-driven shuffle of 'Roll On, Kings Cross' (and its accompanying dub) and the trippy, early morning dub-soul sleaze of 'Dr Doctor', to the minimalist digi-dub brilliance of 'Dr Dub' and Leonidas's cracking Balearic dub pass on 'Roll On, Kings Cross'. … Read more

 Tool B (3:29)
 Review: For his third so release on the Mudline label he established back in 2024, Leo Gibbon has chosen to deliver a "six-track suite" of sample-driven cuts reportedly "pieced together from a balcony while travelling". It certainly sounds sunny and summery, with Gibbon variously blending mid-80s Mediterranean pop synths, evocative ambient chords, warming basslines, manipulated vocal samples, blazed post hip-hop beats, flashes of lifted instrumentation, effects-laden jazz guitar, immersive effects aplenty, tactile deep house grooves, otherworldly electronics and - on standout 'Chapel North' - the spoken rhymes of beat poet James Messiah. It's an impressive mini-album all told, and one tailor-made for relaxed moments and gentle, sun-splashed mornings. … Read more

 Untitled Mix 2 (2:21)
 Review: Copenhagen has inspired plenty of people over the years, and the cohort isn't lacking musicians to use as examples. But something very special must have been in the water during the three days FELT founder Fergus Jones and meditative vocalist Thomas Bush spent in the Danish capital, working on this double-header. It's the first time the label head has appeared on his own imprint, while the singer will already be familiar to fans of that back catalogue. As you would expect, given bossman is involved, the twin gems here are moody, downtempo journeys into dark and strange musical places informed by Balearic, jazz, Arab Strap... you probably still don't quite get the point, so hit play and prepare to be captivated.

 Palmy Daze (Surf Rider mix) (5:04)
 Review: Earlier this year, Swiss Balaeric don Alex "Lexx" Storrer launched a new label, Unlimited Capacity, with the label debuting via a freewheeling, Krautrock-influenced dub disco anthem by the Telemores (the on-point 'No Phones In The Dance'). Storrer handles release number two himself with an EP focused on the more club-ready end of his sound. In its original mix form (A2), 'Palmy Days' is a low-slung, organ-bass propelled chunk of bleeping electronic disco/deep house fusion blessed with a memorable bassline and picturesque melodic motifs. It comes accompanied by two revisions - the gorgeous Balearic breaks shuffle of the Lexx and Jex Opolis take, and the gentle, sun-soaked Balearica of the Surf Rider version - as well as tactile, dreamy and nostalgic deep house jam 'Allure'. … Read more

 Baul Dimension (Roy Vision Higher Self dub) (8:05)
 Review: Born as an audiophile listening party in Paris, the freshly minted Sweet Apricots label promises "music with depth, character and soul" from across a wide range of genres. To kick things off, they've turned to long-serving French musician and producer Daniel Masson, an artist known for blending "world-fusion" sounds with electronics. 'Baul Dimension', which has never been released on vinyl before, dates from 2006 and sees Masson wrap Bangladeshi percussion and strings around a deep, shuffling house groove. Masson provides his own 20th anniversary version, where layered, effects-laden female vocals usher in a deep, dubby and stripped-back groove, before Roy Vision delivers two takes: a drum-rich, slow-build 'Remix' built around percussion solos, spacey synths and flashes of Masson's exotic instrumentation, and a sublime, ambient style 'Higher Self Dub'. … Read more

 Review: Portland's Osmosis crew head up their own much-loved party series and the Osmosis in the Trees festival and now take a familiar next step by branching out with their own label. This six-track opener offers deep, percussive club music threaded with dubwise bass and hypnotic downtempo. Priori's 'Colossi' opens on serious low-end weight at a chuggy 105 BPM, then 'Back Alley' tightens into darker, more intricate percussion at 130. 'Burning Sand' closes the A-side in psychedelic territory as the flip opens with 'Fuul', which leans hard into sub-bass and dub. 'Belladonna' brings gong and gamelan-adjacent textures into something ritualistic, and closer 'Ugamelon Spirit' settles into spacious percussion and a hypnotic wood-flute line. A top transmission from what's clearly a well-curated scene.

 As The Sunsets

 Outro
 Review: Multi-faceted Detroit talent John Beltran brings the Back To Bahia story full circle here. After five years of sun-soaked 7"s that disappeared almost as fast as they dropped, the finest moments are finally gathered on one CD. It's Balearic soul with Motor City heart, glowing percussion, lush synths and that unmistakable Beltran warmth. Each track feels like a postcard from paradise, written in rhythm. 'Bem Vindo' kicks off with florid whistles that make you want to swing open the windows and breath in summer air. 'Laura' is a deeper broken beat sound with radiant synth heat and 'Just Beyond The Sunset' is a shimmering late night sound that's carefully layered with percussion, Latin motifs and dreamy pads. Pure bliss.

 Staff Roll 1 (0:05)
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1164866-01-04-13.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1164866-01-04-13.mp3] 
 Staff Roll 2 (0:05)
 Review: Also known as Vampire Hunter, Night Warriors: Darkstalkers Revenge is not only considered a mid-1990s cult classic video game, but also a landmark of chip music. Once derided by all-but the most avid fans of playable stuff, retro computer game soundtracks have found far wider audiences in the digital age, mirroring the rise of pixel art. The legendary Capcom Sound Team - responsible for scoring many beloved titles produced by the iconic development house - are widely considered titans of this sonic scene. Night Warriors: Darkstalkers Revenge is a prime example of what the Team do so well. It's action packed, highly creative and wildly varied stuff throughout. A reflection of just how important music has been in the process of building playable worlds, especially in the age of limited graphic processing capabilities, if you're at all interested in this niche, this is for you.
 … Read more

 Good Day Will Arrive (2:54)
 Review: American male-female duo Ear made their full-length debut last year with a pleasingly DIY release - a USB memory stick that could only be purchased direct from their website. Second album Rumspringa, which has been committed to wax unlike its predecessor, feels like a subtle evolution of their "laptop twee" style - a sonic mash-up in which their whimsical folk guitars, pastoral instrumentation, lo-fi home recordings and dreamy, wide-eyed vocals are fused with what one critic called "digital detritus", a mix of warped electronics, second-hand drum-machine beats and all manner of crackling, unlikely samples. The resultant musical sound soup is certainly distinctive and - more significantly - really rather good. … Read more

 Everyone (part 2) (6:43)
 Review: Greg Foat - one of the UK's premier jazz pianist-composers - continues to refract work through the facets of his own Blue Crystal label, preferring live-arranged soul jazz through dance music fusions. Everyone comes after his last sonic nonagon of "blobs", moving away from that record's titleless ethos towards a properly-chaptered, Tokyo-born chillout set. Recorded in late September 2025 in the city, it chronicles Foat's first tour of Japan after a commission by the clothing brand of the same name, indulging live saxophone, choppy new age samples and digitally duplicated Balearic percussion. … Read more

 Trust (4:54)
 Review: LA's Nico Leibman is a romantic dream pop multi-instrumentalist who uses her debut album under the Harmony Index alias to bring in some more textural contrast on Peak Oil label FO. Reflecting "the feelings that are most difficult to express" she uses rhythms from breaky-house, trip-hop and lounge to explore desire and surrender. The record was written in phases over the course of the last five years while listening to acts like Everything But The Girl and Janet Jackson and allowing real-world experiences of living in LA to colour the moods, particularly long late-night drives. Euphoria and melancholy sit side by side with Leibman's soft and soulful tones holding everything together and making for a fresh, inventive new twist on her signature sound.

 The Sweet Spot V2 (1:20)
 Review: Adventure Time was one of the highlights of the Adult Swim animated heyday. The studio is still making some amazing series, but as with anything that winds up producing so much content, the differentiating factors slowly become harder to, err, differentiate between, and you wind up slightly more immune to the charms. All of which makes Adventure Time: Fionna & Cake, a spinoff of the original series which debuted in 2023, something of an outlier - it did make an impact and is incredibly memorable. The soundtrack is a big part of the joy, somewhere between Studio Ghibli niceness and action-fantasy video game sounds, the OST release is bound to appeal to avid synth fans everywhere. Props to The Anti-Job's Amanda Jones, then.

 Review: Japanese composer Yoko Kanno's second soundtrack for this series is centred on 2nd GIG and her genre-hopping instincts run wild. 'Cyber Bird', sung by frequent alter ego Gabriela Robin, opens on something eerie and atmospheric, while Origa's 'Rise' carries the season's opening theme. Elsewhere, the record swerves through everything from trip-hop to lounge jazz: Ilaria Graziano lends 'I Can't Be Cool' a smoky, understated cool, and closing theme 'Living Inside the Shell' pairs Steve Conte and Shanti Snyder for one of the collection's most emotionally direct moments. 17 tracks and Kanno never repeats herself, which is why this is one of anime's most celebrated scores. … Read more

 Forgiven (3:02)
 Review: Thanks to the success of her 2024 debut album, Mother, and last year's contribution to the iconic DJ Kicks series, Logic 100 is big news. The Berlin-based Aussie is at her musical best on sophomore set Confirmation! (the exclamation part is important), delivering a suite of attractive, melody-rich cuts that fuse deep, classy synth-pop sounds with woozy, manipulated samples, maximalist sound design and just enough dancefloor-ready grunt to please DJs. There's not enough space to list all the highlights, but our picks of a very strong bunch include the mid-80s big studio AOR synth-pop dreaminess of Christine & The Queens collaboration 'The Only One', Jam City hook-up 'Jersey' (a tactile and loved-up late-night delight) and 'All In For You', a breakbeat-driven chunk of Balearic dancefloor-pop headiness featuring her self-proclaimed "vocal crush", KUCKA. … Read more

 Molly & Kazmyn - "Here Again" (Molly rework) (4:22)
 Review: Some readers may not have heard of Molly, a Californian yoga teacher turned producer of melody-driven deep and progressive house. Yet in some corners of dance music culture, she is a huge name - as a staggering 20 million plays on streaming services in less than two years shows. This debut album, then, is big news. Now finally available on vinyl in lightly expanded form, it begins with a classy piece of meditative piano ambient and ends with a fresh rework of 2024 tactile vocal deep house number 'Here Again' (with Kazymyn). In between, you'll find a wealth of dreamy collaborative cuts that sit somewhere in between the expansive, musically layered beauty of All Day I Dream's brand of deep progressive house, the more ethereal and relaxed end of progressive trance, and the Autotune-sporting world of radio-ready house-pop. … Read more

 Use Your Noggin (3:59)
 Review: There's a joke buried in the title here: OOO stands for Out Of Office, and the Utrecht trio behind .multibeat say they almost never were one while making it, instead holed up together chasing the kind of live groove that a day job doesn't allow. Boudewijn Pleij, Thijs van Zutphen and Aron Smit have built a sound over two albums that mix jazz, hip-hop and neo-soul in real time. Their Sonar Kollektiv debut leans on a wide cast to fill it out further. Sam Wilder and Casiio bring warmth to 'Please Hold Me' and 'Hold Me Down,' Neema Nekesa and Noflik turn up elsewhere and longtime collaborator Paul van de Calseijde helps anchor 'Bloom' as the record's soulful centre. 12 tracks explore several different moods but all of them are complementary. … Read more

 Flutter (8:00)
 Review: nthng is already well known for this meticulous productions that thread IDM, techno, dub and drum & bass and his fifth album, Last Light of the Sun, his debut on topo2, only backs up that reputation. It is a subtle listen for sinking into from the off. Tracks such as 'Orchid' capture that balance particularly well, while the closing 'Flutter' drifts into a spacious, almost dreamlike territory that recalls the melodic ambience of David August. There are moments when a little melancholy has a huge emotional impact, but there is also a strong refusal to overstate things. Last Light of the Sun is music that settles in and lingers. … Read more

 The Orb - "Phone Chat Line"
 Review: It was way back in 1996 that Dr Alex Paterson first decided to explore his vast catalogue of remixes as the Orb, offering up the seminal Auntie Aubrey Excursions compilation. A sequel arrived in 2001, but nothing since. Given that 25 years have passed, this third missive is well overdue. While there are some unreleased and extremely hard to find revisions from the 90s present - see the opening rub of Coldcut and Hex's 'Timber' - for the most part this is later period Orb, where the usual ambient house and ambient dub exploits sit side by side with dubby and minimal techno and plenty of pleasingly psychedelic workouts - remixes aplenty for Youth's Liquid Sound Design label, of course, but also rubs of old pals System 7, ambient survivor and Weatherall collaborator David Harrow, Francesco Lombardo and the Transit Kings (itself an Orb offshoot). … Read more

 The Orb - "Phone Chat Line"
 Review: Orb fans can rejoice because Auntie Aubrey's Part 3 revives the Orb Remix Project series after nearly two decades. It's a sprawling collection that spans three decades of Alex Paterson's playfully subversive remixes, from a melancholy rework of Transit Kings' 'The Last Lighthouse Keeper' - surely the only track to feature both The KLF's Jimmy Cauty AND Simon Day of The Fast Show fame - to System 7's sun-dappled 'Davy Jones' Locker' and Killing Joke's spy-thriller-esque 'European Super State,' featuring Youth on bass. Youth also teams up with Lx for a psychedelic cut fronted by Lara Smiles. Further contributions from Coldcut, David Harrow, Sendelica, Gaudi, DF Tram and more make this a collector's treasure trove whether you're an Orb devotee or serious ambient head … Read more

 Pink (2:15)
 Review: UK-based Duval Timothy is a pianist by origin, but his fourth album Help (2020) took on an avant-garde direction, working internet-ripped sound effect and healing sounds to a limitless bed of experimental beats. Documenting Timothy's own time spent navigating music industry troubles, all while lending his attention to self-help YouTube videos (which don't help), this 18-tracker was made in loving hindsight for that time, looping in artists Lil Silva, Mr Mitch, Vegyn and Melanie Faye for an elaborate. Where solo piano doesn't dominate (like on 'U'), there's stretched mains hum, jazz texture, birdsong, 80s arpeggio and glittering glass. … Read more
